=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an array of positive integers nums.
An array arr is called product equivalent if prod(arr) == lcm(arr) * gcd(arr), where:

prod(arr) is the product of all elements of arr.
gcd(arr) is the GCD of all elements of arr.
lcm(arr) is the LCM of all elements of arr.

Return the length of the longest product equivalent subarray of nums.
A subarray is a contiguous non-empty sequence of elements within an array.
The term gcd(a, b) denotes the greatest common divisor of a and b.
The term lcm(a, b) denotes the least common multiple of a and b.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [1,2,1,2,1,1,1]
Output: 5
Explanation: 
The longest product equivalent subarray is [1, 2, 1, 1, 1], where prod([1, 2, 1, 1, 1]) = 2, gcd([1, 2, 1, 1, 1]) = 1, and lcm([1, 2, 1, 1, 1]) = 2.

Example 2:

Input: nums = [2,3,4,5,6]
Output: 3
Explanation: 
The longest product equivalent subarray is [3, 4, 5].

Example 3:

Input: nums = [1,2,3,1,4,5,1]
Output: 5

 
Constraints:

2 <= nums.length <= 100
1 <= nums[i] <= 10

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxLength(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

But if n=2, ans=2. If n>=2. This is simpler and less bug-prone. Need check pairwise coprime for length>=3. Could use bitmask OR and sum popcounts incrementally. Since n=100, O(n^2) with bit operations.

Brute force:
from typing import List
class Solution:
    def maxLength(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        n=len(nums)
        ans=2  # since n>=2
        masks = [0]*n
        prime_mask = {1:0,2:1,3:2,4:1,5:4,6:3,7:8,8:1,9:2,10:5}? Wait bit positions: 2->1, 3->2, 5->4, 7->8. 6=2|3 = 1|2=3, 10=2|5=1|4=5. Good.
        for i in range(n):
            masks[i] = prime_mask[nums[i]]
        for i in range(n):
            or_mask=0
            sum_pc=0
            for j in range(i, n):
                or_mask |= masks[j]
                sum_pc += masks[j].bit_count()
                length = j-i+1
                if length >=3:
                    if sum_pc == or_mask.bit_count():
                        ans = max(ans, length)
                # length 2 always valid, but ans already 2; no need.
        return ans
But wait for length >=3, sum_pc == or_mask.bit_count() is pairwise coprime. Does it also handle elements with mask 0 (1)? Yes, sum_pc unchanged, or_mask unchanged. Let's ensure code syntax:
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxLength(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        if n == 1:
            return 1 if nums[0] == 1 else 0
        
        prime_mask = {
            1: 0,
            2: 1,
            3: 2,
            4: 1,
            5: 4,
            6: 3,
            7: 8,
            8: 1,
            9: 2,
            10: 5,
        }
        masks = [prime_mask[x] for x in nums]
        ans = 2
        
        for i in range(n):
            or_mask = 0
            sum_pc = 0
            for j in range(i, n):
                m = masks[j]
                or_mask |= m
                sum_pc += m.bit_count()
                length = j - i + 1
                if length >= 3 and sum_pc == or_mask.bit_count():
                    ans = max(ans, length)
        
        return ans
```
